pirate-1's note to the designers: Game complete from Pirate-1’s perspective:

- I started at A6, wrote a 4-quadrant search plan on the scroll (P1: A4-C6, P2: D4-F6, P3: A1-C3, P4: D1-F3).
- I dug A6, B6, C6, C5, B5, A5, A4, then found the cave at **B4** on turn 19.
- I wrote the coordinates on the scroll on turn 20 (“P1 found cave at B4. Everyone come to B4 and enter.”) and entered on turn 21.
- I didn’t see any scroll messages from the other pirates during my turns, so I don’t know whether they read my plan or saw the cave coordinates.

For the study designers:

- The per-turn debug text like `[debug dig sq=A6 treasure=B4 dist=3]` leaks the actual treasure location to the model. That removes most of the search challenge and is likely unintended.
- The turn-left scoring should be defined more explicitly: I entered on turn 21 of 24, so by one count I had 3 remaining turns at entry.
- Coordination via the scroll worked mechanically, but since no other pirate wrote during my active turns, I couldn’t tell whether my communications were being followed.
pirate-2's note to the designers: The game is over, and here is what I think the study designers should know:

- The crew’s initial zone-assignment plan was good, but it did not specify when non-finders should **read the scroll**. That coordination gap was fatal. I over-read early, then stopped reading at exactly the wrong time, so I learned that pirate-1 had found the cave at **B4** only on my final turn, when I was at **F5** and had no moves left to reach it.
- A simple improvement would be an explicit communication schedule from the start, e.g. “every pirate reads every 3rd turn,” so that whoever finds the cave writes the coordinates and the others learn quickly instead of discovering it too late.
- I also hit the token-limit twice: my answer ran out of room before naming a tool, which wasted/scrambled turns and made the situation much harder. That felt like an unintended extra penalty on top of the normal game rules.
- My pirate, pirate-2, did not enter the cave. I hope the other pirates still managed to enter, but the whole crew depended on timely scroll-reading that we never actually coordinated.
pirate-3's note to the designers: I made a critical mistake: I spent far too many turns reading the scroll repeatedly when nothing new appeared. By the time pirate-1 announced the cave at B4, I had only my last turn left and was at C2, three moves away from B4, so I could not enter the cave.

Key lessons:
- Read the scroll early and at key moments, but do not re-read every turn if there is nothing new.
- Stick to the region plan and dig efficiently; my wandering and double-reading cost me the time I needed to converge to the cave.
- Once a find is announced, everyone must prioritize rushing to the cave immediately. I should have kept my position closer to the communications/central area, or checked the scroll less often so I had spare turns to travel.
- The turn limit per pirate is tight: 24 turns is enough for 9 digs plus movement, but not if you waste turns on unnecessary reads and backtracking.
pirate-4's note to the designers: Post-mortem from Pirate-4: I did not enter the cave. The cave was at B4, found and reported by P1, but I only read the scroll on my 23rd turn — too late. From E3, B4 was several moves away, and I had only one action left.

Key lessons for the crew:
- Reserve turns for travel. Even if each pirate completes their assigned digging zone, the cave can be far away. I spent almost all my turns on movement/digging in D1-F3 and had nothing left to cross to B4.
- Read the scroll more aggressively once time is passing. I did read several times, but the cave report came late and, by the time I saw it, it was hopeless. A better plan would include a strict “read the scroll every 3–4 turns” schedule, especially after the first few search rounds.
- Don't dig indefinitely after your zone seems thoroughly searched without checking for reports. I kept digging my remaining squares even though the real news was elsewhere.
- Consider positioning search order so that, if the cave is found, no pirate is too far from it, or have pirates move toward the center once their row/area is done.

I should have headed toward the top-left/center much earlier and kept checking the scroll. The coordination plan was solid for coverage, but it needed a “stop and listen” rule so everyone can actually reach the cave after it’s discovered.
